````math
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{H}\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)\frac{16C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}\left(2C_{\mathsf{n}}+\sqrt{C_{\mathsf{n}}C_{3}}\right)H\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K\min\left\{d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b),\frac{1}{S(A+B)}\right\}}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{32C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}\left(2C_{\mathsf{n}}+\sqrt{C_{\mathsf{n}}C_{3}}\right)H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K},`$ |  | (75) |

where the inequality holds by the trivial fact

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}\frac{d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)}{\min\left\{d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b),\frac{1}{S(A+B)}\right\}}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)\left(\frac{1}{d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)}+\frac{1}{1/S(A+B)}\right)`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}1+S(A+B)\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)\leq 2S(A+B).`$ |  | (76) |

#### D.2.3 Putting all together

Combining
([74](#A4.Ex61 "In D.2.1 Bounding ∑_{𝑖=1}^𝐻∑_{(𝑠,𝑏)∈𝒮×ℬ}{𝑑_𝑖^{𝗉,𝜇^𝖽,𝜈^⋆}⁢(𝑠,𝜇^𝖽⁢(𝑠),𝑏)⁢𝐵_1} ‣ D.2 Step 2: Decomposing the error Gap(𝜇̂,𝜈̂) ‣ Appendix D Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
and
([75](#A4.Ex63 "In D.2.2 Bounding ∑_{𝑖=1}^𝐻∑_{(𝑠,𝑏)∈𝒮×ℬ}{𝑑_𝑖^{𝗉,𝜇^𝖽,𝜈^⋆}⁢(𝑠,𝜇^𝖽⁢(𝑠),𝑏)⁢𝐵_2} ‣ D.2 Step 2: Decomposing the error Gap(𝜇̂,𝜈̂) ‣ Appendix D Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
we obtain

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{H}\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)\beta_{i}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b,\widehat{V})`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{128C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{\mathsf{n}}H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\times\sqrt{\left(4\sum_{i=1}^{H}\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)\beta_{i}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b,\widehat{V})+(H+3)\right)}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{32C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}\left(2C_{\mathsf{n}}+\sqrt{C_{\mathsf{n}}C_{3}}\right)H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K},`$ |  | (77) |

which can further bound as

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{H}\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)\beta_{i}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b,\widehat{V})`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{128C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{\mathsf{n}}H^{2}(H+3)S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{32C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}\left(2C_{\mathsf{n}}+\sqrt{C_{\mathsf{n}}C_{3}}\right)H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}+\sqrt{\frac{512C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{\mathsf{n}}H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\times\sqrt{\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}\sum_{i=1}^{H}\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)\beta_{i}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b,\widehat{V})}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{128C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{\mathsf{n}}H^{2}(H+3)S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{32C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}\left(2C_{\mathsf{n}}+\sqrt{C_{\mathsf{n}}C_{3}}\right)H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{256C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{\mathsf{n}}H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{H}\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)\beta_{i}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b,\widehat{V}),`$ |  | (78) |

where the last relation follows from the AM-GM inequality. Rearranging
the terms, it follows that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{H}\sum_{(s,b)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{B}}d_{i}^{\sf{p},\mu^{\sf{d}},\nu^{\star}}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b)\beta_{i}(s,\mu^{\sf{d}}(s),b,\widehat{V})`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{512C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{\mathsf{n}}H^{2}(H+3)S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{64C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}\left(2C_{\mathsf{n}}+\sqrt{C_{\mathsf{n}}C_{3}}\right)H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{512C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{\mathsf{n}}H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{512C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{\mathsf{n}}H^{2}(H+3)S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{2}H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}.`$ |  | (79) |

Along with the above analysis, we are ready to bound
$`{V}_{1}^{\star,\sigma^{+}}(\varrho)-{V}_{1}^{\widehat{\mu},\star,\sigma^{+}}(\varrho)`$:
There exist some sufficiently large constants $`C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0`$,
and

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{1}^{\star,\widehat{\nu},\sigma^{+}}(\varrho)-V_{1}^{\star,\sigma^{+}}(\varrho)\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{1}H^{3}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{2}H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{3}H^{3}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}{\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},H\right\}}},`$ |  | (80) |

where the last inequality follows from condition
([22](#S4.E22 "In Theorem 4.2 (Upper bound for RTZ-VI-LCB). ‣ Robust unilateral clipped concentrability. ‣ 4 Performance Guarantees ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")).

### D.3 Step 3: Summing up

Consequently, we obtain the upper bound of
$`V_{1}^{\star,\widehat{\nu},\sigma^{+}}(\varrho)-V_{1}^{\widehat{\mu},\widehat{\nu},\sigma^{+}}(\varrho)`$
in
([80](#A4.Ex82 "In D.2.3 Putting all together ‣ D.2 Step 2: Decomposing the error Gap(𝜇̂,𝜈̂) ‣ Appendix D Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")).
Similarly,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle V_{1}^{\star,\sigma^{-}}(\varrho)-V_{1}^{\widehat{\mu},\star,\sigma^{-}}(\varrho)`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}C_{3}H^{2}S(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}\min\left\{\frac{(H+1)(H\sigma^{-}-1+(1-\sigma^{-})^{H})}{(\sigma^{-})^{2}},H\right\}},`$ |  | (81) |

which directly leads to

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{Gap}(\widehat{\mu},\widehat{\nu})\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\frac{C_{\mathsf{r}}^{\star}H^{2}S(A\!+\!B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{K}}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\times\sqrt{\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},\frac{2(H\sigma^{-}-1+(1-\sigma^{-})^{H})}{(\sigma^{-})^{2}},H\right\}},`$ |  | (82) |

for some sufficiently large $`c_{1}`$ and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
K\geq HS(A+B)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}\min\left\{\frac{2(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},\frac{2(H\sigma^{-}-1+(1-\sigma^{-})^{H})}{(\sigma^{-})^{2}},H\right\}.
``` |  |

##### Discussion of ([82](#A4.Ex85 "In D.3 Step 3: Summing up ‣ Appendix D Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")).

For the term $`T=\min\left(f(\sigma^{+},\sigma^{-}),H\right)`$,
considering the interchangeability between $`\sigma^{+}`$ and
$`\sigma^{-}`$, we define
$`g(\sigma^{+},H)=H\sigma^{+}-H(1-\sigma^{+})^{H}-(\sigma^{+})^{2}H`$.
For $`H\geq 2`$, we derive the first derivative as
$`\frac{\partial g(\sigma^{+},H)}{\partial\sigma^{+}}=H+H^{2}(1-\sigma^{+})^{H-1}-2H\sigma^{+}`$.
The second derivative is given by
$`\frac{\partial^{2}g(\sigma^{+},H)}{\partial(\sigma^{+})^{2}}=-H^{2}(H-1)(1-\sigma^{+})^{H-2}-2H<0`$,
indicating that $`g(\sigma^{+},H)`$ is concave. By evaluating the first
derivative at the boundaries, we find
$`\frac{\partial g(\sigma^{+},H)}{\partial\sigma^{+}}|_{\sigma^{+}\to 0}\to H^{2}+H>0`$
and
$`\frac{\partial g(\sigma^{+},H)}{\partial\sigma^{+}}|_{\sigma^{+}=1}=-H<0`$,
showing that $`g(\sigma^{+},H)`$ first increases monotonically, reaches
a maximum at some point $`\sigma^{\star}`$, and then decreases
monotonically. Furthermore, since $`g(\sigma^{+}\to 0,H)\to-H<0`$ and
$`g(\sigma^{+}=1,H)=0`$, there exists $`0<\sigma^{0}<1`$ such that
$`g(\sigma^{0},H)=0`$. If
$`\sigma^{0}\lesssim\min\{\sigma^{+},\sigma^{-}\}\lesssim 1`$, we have
$`T=H`$. Otherwise,
$`T=\min\left\{\frac{(H\sigma^{+}-1+(1-\sigma^{+})^{H})}{(\sigma^{+})^{2}},\frac{(H\sigma^{-}-1+(1-\sigma^{-})^{H})}{(\sigma^{-})^{2}}\right\}`$.

## Appendix E Proof of Theorem [4.4](#S4.Thmtheorem4 "Theorem 4.4 (Lower bound for RTZMGs). ‣ Robust unilateral clipped concentrability. ‣ 4 Performance Guarantees ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")

We focus on a simple class of RTZMGs: robust Markov decision processes
(RMDPs), which are single-agent versions of RTZMGs. Recall that an RTZMG
with an uncertainty set is
$`\mathcal{MG}=\{\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{0}),\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{-}}(P^{0}),r,H\}`$.
For illustration convenience, we assume $`\mathcal{A}\geq\mathcal{B}`$
and $`|\mathcal{B}|=1`$, meaning the min-player’s actions do not affect
transitions or rewards. Thus, finding a robust NE in RTZMGs reduces to
finding the max-player’s optimal policy in a corresponding RMDP
$`\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{r}}=\{\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{0}),r,H\}`$.

Thus, we construct the lower bound for finding the optimal policy in
RTZMGs, which also implies a lower bound for finding robust NE in
RTZMGs. We start by re-stating a useful property about KL divergence
from Lemma 2.7 in \[[36](#bib.bib2)\].

###### Lemma E.1.

$`\forall p,q\in(0,1)`$, it holds that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(p\parallel q)\leq\frac{(p-q)^{2}}{q(1-q)}.`$ |  | (83) |

### E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances

The hard instances developed here differ from standard MDP since we need
to consider that the transition kernel can be perturbed in robust MDPs.

#### E.1.1 Constructing hard robust MDP instances

We introduce an auxiliary collection $`\Phi\subseteq\{0,1\}^{H}`$
comprising $`H`$-dimensional vectors. Resorting to the Gilbert-Varshamov
lemma \[[15](#bib.bib34)\], there also exists a set
$`\Phi\subseteq\{0,1\}^{H}`$ such that:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\text{for any }\phi,\widetilde{\phi}\in\Phi\text{ obeying }\phi\neq\widetilde{\phi}:\quad\|\phi-\widetilde{\phi}\|_{1}\geq\frac{H}{8}\qquad\text{and}\qquad|\Phi|\geq e^{H/8}.
``` |  | (84) |

Bearing this in mind, we construct a set of RMDPs as

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)\!\coloneqq\!\Bigg\{\!\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi}\!=\!\big(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{f,\phi}),r,H\big)\!\mid`$ | $`\displaystyle f\!\in\!\mathcal{F}\!=\!\{0,1,\cdots,SA-1\},\phi=[\phi_{h}]_{1\leq h\leq H}\!\in\!\Phi\Bigg\},`$ |  | (85) |

where $`\mathcal{S}=\{0,1,\ldots,S-1\}`$,
$`\mathcal{A}=\{0,1,\cdots,A-1\}`$, and $`\sigma^{+}`$ will be
introduced momentarily.

In simple terms, the collection $`\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)`$
consists of $`SA`$ subsets, each containing $`|\Phi|`$ different RMDPs
associated with some $`f\in\mathcal{F}`$. The state space for each RMDP
$`\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)`$, denoted as
$`\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}`$, includes two types of states:
$`\mathcal{M}=\{m_{i}\mid i\in\mathcal{F}\}`$ and
$`\mathcal{N}=\{n_{i}\mid i\in\mathcal{F}\}`$. Each state in
$`\mathcal{M}`$ and $`\mathcal{N}`$ has two possible actions,
$`\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}}=\{0,1\}`$. Thus, there are a total of
$`2SA`$ states and $`4SA`$ state-action pairs.

Now, we can define the transition kernels for
$`\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)`$. For any RMDP
$`\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)`$, the
transition kernel $`P^{f,\phi}=\{P^{f,\phi}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}`$ is defined
as follows, for any
$`(s,a,s^{\prime},h)\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times[H]`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle P^{f,\phi}_{h}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}p\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=n_{f})+(1-p)\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=s),&\text{if}\quad s=m_{f},a=\phi_{h};\\
q\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=n_{f})+(1-q)\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=s),&\text{if}\quad s=m_{f},a=1-\phi_{h};\\
\ind(s^{\prime}=s),&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.`$ |  |

where $`p>q\geq\frac{1}{2}`$.

In addition, the reward function is defined as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall(h,s,a)\in[H]\times\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}}:\quad r_{h}(s,a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}1,&\text{if }s\in\mathcal{N};\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.`$ |  |

#### E.1.2 Uncertainty set of the transition kernels

Denote the transition kernel vector as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall(h,s,a)\in[H]\times\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}}:\quad P_{h,s,a}^{f,\phi}\coloneqq P^{f,\phi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)\in\Delta(\mathcal{S}).`$ |  | (91) |

Recall the uncertainty set defined in
([1](#S2.E1 "In Uncertainty set with two-player (s,a,b)-rectangularity. ‣ 2 Problem Formulation ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")).
$`\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{f,\phi})`$ represents

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{f,\phi})\coloneqq\otimes\;\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a}),\quad`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a})\coloneqq\Big\{\widetilde{P}^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a}\in\Delta(\mathcal{S}):\rho\left(\widetilde{P}^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a}-P^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a}\right)\leq\sigma^{+}\Big\},`$ |  |

where $`\otimes`$ represents the Cartesian product over
$`(h,s,a)\in[H]\times\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}}`$.
For the convenience of the subsequent proof, we analyze the TV distance
as an uncertainty set for example, which means

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a})\coloneqq\Big\{\widetilde{P}^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a}\in\Delta(\mathcal{S}):\frac{1}{2}\left\|\widetilde{P}^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a}-P^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a}\right\|\leq\sigma^{+}\Big\}.`$ |  | (92) |

For any RMDP $`\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)`$
and any
$`(h,s,a,s^{\prime})\in[H]\times\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}`$,
we define the minimum transition probability from $`(s,a)`$ to
$`s^{\prime}`$, determined by any perturbed transition kernel
$`P_{h,s,a}\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a})`$, as

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle{P}_{h}^{\inf,f,\phi}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)`$ | $`\displaystyle\coloneqq\inf_{P_{h,s,a}\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(P^{f,\phi}_{h,s,a})}P_{h}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)=\max\{P_{h}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)-\sigma^{+},0\},`$ |  | (93) |

where the last equation inherits from the definition of
$`\mathcal{U}^{\sigma^{+}}(\cdot)`$ in
([92](#A5.E92 "In E.1.2 Uncertainty set of the transition kernels ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
with the remaining probability distributed to other states. We also
define the transition from each $`s\in\mathcal{M}`$ to the corresponding
state $`s^{m\rightarrow n}\in\mathcal{N}`$ for any
$`\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi}`$: for all $`h\in[H]`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\text{for }m_{f}:\quad`$ | $`\displaystyle{p}_{h}^{\inf}\coloneqq{P}_{h}^{\inf,f,\phi}(n_{f}\,|\,m_{f},\phi_{h})=p-\sigma^{+},`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle{q}_{h}^{\inf}\coloneqq{P}_{h}^{\inf,f,\phi}(n_{f}\,|\,m_{f},1-\phi_{h})=q-\sigma^{+}.`$ |  | (94) |

It is obvious that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle{p}^{\inf}_{1}={p}^{\inf}_{2}=\cdots{p}^{\inf}_{H},`$ | $`\displaystyle\quad{q}^{\inf}_{1}={q}^{\inf}_{2}=\cdots{q}^{\inf}_{H},`$ |  | (95) |

which motivates us to abbreviate them consistently as
$`{p}^{\inf}\coloneqq{p}^{\inf}_{1}`$ and
$`{q}^{\inf}\coloneqq{q}^{\inf}_{1}`$ later.

#### E.1.3 Robust value functions and optimal policies

We now define the robust value functions and identify the optimal
policies for RMDP instances. For any RMDP
$`\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)`$, let
$`\widetilde{\mu}^{\star,f,\phi}=\{\mu^{\star,f,\phi}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}`$
represent the optimal policy, given that $`\nu`$ is deterministic. At
each step $`h`$, we use $`V_{h}^{\widetilde{\mu},\sigma^{+},f,\phi}`$
and $`V_{h}^{\star,\sigma^{+},f,\phi}`$ to denote the robust value
function of any policy $`\widetilde{\mu}`$ and the optimal policy
$`\widetilde{\mu}^{\star,f,\phi}`$, respectively, under uncertainty
level $`\sigma^{+}`$. The following lemma highlights key properties of
robust value functions and optimal policies; the proof is deferred to
Appendix [H.1](#A8.SS1 "H.1 Proof of Lemma ‣ Appendix H Proof for Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning").

###### Lemma E.2.

Consider any $`\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)`$
and any policy $`\widetilde{\mu}`$. Defining

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle m_{h}^{\widetilde{\mu},f,\phi}={p}^{\inf}\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\phi_{h}\,|\,m_{f})+{q}^{\inf}\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(1-\phi_{h}\,|\,m_{f}),`$ |  | (96) |

it holds that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  |  |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall h\in[H]:\quad`$ | $`\displaystyle V_{h}^{\widetilde{\mu},\sigma^{+},f,\phi}(m_{f})=m_{h}^{\widetilde{\mu},f,\phi}V_{h+1}^{\widetilde{\mu},\sigma^{+},f,\phi}(n_{f})+(1-m_{h}^{\widetilde{\mu},f,\phi})V_{h+1}^{\widetilde{\mu},\sigma^{+},f,\phi}(m_{f}),`$ |  | (97a) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall(s,h)\in\mathcal{N}\times[H]:\quad`$ | $`\displaystyle V_{h}^{\widetilde{\mu},\sigma^{+},f,\phi}(s)=1+(1-\sigma^{+})V_{h+1}^{\widetilde{\mu},\sigma^{+},f,\phi}(s)+\sigma^{+}V_{h+1}^{\widetilde{\mu},\sigma^{+},f,\phi}(m_{f}).`$ |  | (97b) |

In addition, for all $`h\in[H]`$, the optimal policy and the optimal
value function obey

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\phi_{h}\,|\,m_{f})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\phi_{h}\,|\,n_{f})=1,`$ |  | (98) |
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{h}^{\star,\sigma^{+},f,\phi}(m_{f})`$ | $`\displaystyle={p}^{\inf}V_{h+1}^{\widetilde{\mu},\sigma^{+},f,\phi}(n_{f})+(1-{p}^{\inf})V_{h+1}^{\widetilde{\mu},\sigma^{+},f,\phi}(m_{f}).`$ |  | (99) |

#### E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset

In the nominal environment $`\mathcal{M}^{\phi,\mathsf{n}}_{f}`$, a
batch dataset is generated with $`K`$ independent sample trajectories
with length $`H`$ per trajectory, according to
([3](#S2.E3 "In Offline dataset. ‣ 2 Problem Formulation ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
and based on the initial state distribution $`\varrho^{\mathsf{n}}`$ and
behavior policy
$`\widetilde{\mu}^{\mathsf{n}}=\{\mu^{\mathsf{n}}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}`$
satisfying

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\varrho^{\mathsf{n}}(s)={\varrho}(s)\quad\text{and }\quad\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\mathsf{n}}(a\,|\,s)=\frac{1}{2},\qquad\forall(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}}\times[H].`$ |  | (100) |

We define the nominal transition kernels for
$`\mathcal{M}^{\phi,\mathsf{n}}_{f}`$, where any state
$`m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}`$ transitions only to the corresponding
$`n_{i}\in\mathcal{N}`$ or remains at itself. For simplicity, for any
$`s=m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}`$, we denote the corresponding state
$`n_{i}\in\mathcal{N}`$ as $`s^{m\rightarrow n}`$. The basic nominal
transition kernel is defined as: For all
$`(h,s,a)\in[H]\times\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle P^{\star}_{h}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}(p+\Delta)\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=s^{m\rightarrow n})+(1-p-\Delta)\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=s),&\text{if}&s\in\mathcal{M},a=\phi_{h};\\
p\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=s^{m\rightarrow n})+(1-p)\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=s),&\text{if}&s\in\mathcal{M},a=1-\phi_{h};\\
\ind(s^{\prime}=s),&\text{if}&s\in\mathcal{N}.\end{array}\right.`$ |  |

In other words, the transition kernel of each
$`\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)`$ differs
slightly from the basic nominal transition kernel
$`\mathcal{M}^{\phi,\mathsf{n}}_{f}`$ when $`s=m_{f}`$, making all
components within $`\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F},\Phi)`$ close to each other.
Specifically, $`p`$ and $`q`$ are set according to

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 0\leq p\leq p+\Delta\leq 1\text{ and }0\leq q=p-\Delta\text{ for some }p,\Delta>0.`$ |  | (104) |

Without loss of generality, let $`\sigma^{+}\in(0,1-c_{0}]`$ for some
$`0<c_{0}<1`$ be the uncertainty level. Taking $`c_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}`$
and $`c_{1}\coloneqq\frac{c_{0}}{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}`$, $`p`$ and
$`\Delta`$ are set as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle p=\begin{cases}\frac{c_{2}}{H},&\text{if }\sigma^{+}\leq\frac{c_{2}}{2H}\\
\left(1+\frac{c_{1}}{H}\right)\sigma^{+}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Delta\leq\begin{cases}\frac{c_{2}}{2H},&\text{if }\sigma^{+}\leq\frac{c_{2}}{2H}\\
\frac{c_{1}}{H}\sigma^{+}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}`$ |  | (105) |

which establishes the fact that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle p+\Delta\geq p\geq q=p-\Delta\geq\max\left\{\frac{c_{2}}{2H},\sigma^{+}\right\}.`$ |  | (106) |

Combined with $`H\geq 2`$, it is easily verified that
$`0\leq p+\Delta\leq 1`$ as follows:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\text{when $\sigma^{+}>\frac{c_{2}}{2H}$}:\quad\left(1+\frac{c_{1}}{H}\right)\sigma^{+}+\frac{c_{1}}{H}\sigma^{+}\leq 1-c_{0}+\frac{2c_{1}}{H}\sigma^{+}\leq 1-\frac{c_{0}(H-1)}{H}<1,`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\text{when $\sigma^{+}\leq\frac{c_{2}}{2H}$}:\quad\frac{3c_{2}}{2H}\leq 1.`$ |  | (107) |

In addition, let $`\overline{\varrho}(s)`$ represent a state
distribution supported on the state subset
$`(m_{f},n_{f})\in\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\overline{\varrho}(s)=\frac{1}{CSA}\mathds{1}(s=m_{f})+\Big(1-\frac{1}{CSA}\Big)\mathds{1}(s=n_{f}),`$ |  | (108) |

where $`\mathds{1}(\cdot)`$ is the indicator function, and $`C>0`$ is
some constant that determines the concentrability coefficient
$`C^{\star}_{\mathrm{r}}`$ (as we shall detail momentarily) and obeys

|     |                                                |     |       |
|-----|------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{CSA}\leq\frac{1}{4}.`$ |     | (109) |

As it turns out, for any MDP $`\mathcal{M}_{\phi}^{f}`$, the occupancy
distributions of the above batch dataset are the same (due to
interchangeability) and admit the following simple characterization:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  |  |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\forall(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}},\qquad\qquad\qquad d^{\mathsf{n},P^{\phi,f}}_{1}(s,a)=\frac{1}{2}\overline{\varrho}(s),`$ |  | (110a) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\forall(s,a,h)\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{one}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{one}}\times[H],\qquad\frac{\overline{\varrho}(s)}{2}\leq d^{\mathsf{n},P^{\phi,f}}_{h}(s)\leq 2\overline{\varrho}(s),\quad\frac{\overline{\varrho}(s)}{4}\leq d^{\mathsf{n},P^{\phi,f}}_{h}(s,a)\leq\overline{\varrho}(s).`$ |  | (110b) |

In addition, we choose the following initial state distribution

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\varrho(s)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{CSA},\quad&\text{if }s\in\mathcal{M}\\
0,&\text{if }s\in\mathcal{N}.\end{cases}`$ |  | (111) |

With this choice of $`\varrho`$, the single-policy clipped
concentrability coefficient $`C^{\star}_{\mathrm{r}}`$ and the quantity
$`C`$ are intimately connected:

|     |                                                        |     |       |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     | $`\displaystyle C\leq C^{\star}_{\mathrm{r}}\leq 2C.`$ |     | (112) |

The proofs of
([110](#A5.E110 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
and
([112](#A5.E112 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
are postponed to
Appendix [H.2](#A8.SS2 "H.2 Proof of () ‣ Appendix H Proof for Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")
and
[H.3](#A8.SS3 "H.3 Proof of () ‣ Appendix H Proof for Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
respectively.

### E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound

Recall our goal: for any policy estimator $`\widetilde{\mu}`$ computed
based on the empirical dataset, we plan to control the quantity

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\max_{(f,\phi)\in\mathcal{F}\times\Phi}\left\{V_{1}^{\star,{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(\varrho)-V_{1}^{\widetilde{\mu},{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(\varrho)\right\}`$ |  | (113) |

with initial state distribution defined in
([111](#A5.E111 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")).

#### E.2.1 Step 1: Converting the goal to estimate $`(f,\phi)`$

As verified in
Appendix [H.4](#A8.SS4 "H.4 Proof of () ‣ Appendix H Proof for Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\varepsilon\leq\begin{cases}\frac{c_{2}}{H},&\text{if }{\sigma^{+}}\leq\frac{c_{2}}{2H};\\
1,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}`$ |  | (114) |

and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Delta=c_{5}\begin{cases}\frac{\varepsilon}{H^{2}},&\text{if }{\sigma^{+}}\leq\frac{c_{2}}{2H};\\
\frac{{\sigma^{+}}\varepsilon}{H},&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}`$ |  | (115) |

which satisfies
([105](#A5.E105 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
and leads to that for any policy $`\widetilde{\mu}`$ obeying

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}\geq\frac{H}{8},`$ |  | (116) |

one has

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{1}^{\star,{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(m_{f})-V_{1}^{\widetilde{\mu},{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(m_{f})>\varepsilon,`$ |  | (117) |

whose proof is postponed to
Appendix [H.4](#A8.SS4 "H.4 Proof of () ‣ Appendix H Proof for Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning").
Now, we are ready to convert the estimation of an optimal policy to
estimating $`(f,\phi)`$. Let $`\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}`$ represent the
probability distribution when the RMDP is
$`\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi},\,\forall(f,\phi)\in\mathcal{F}\times\Phi`$.
For any $`(f,\phi)\in\mathcal{F}\times\Phi`$, suppose that there exists
a policy $`\widetilde{\mu}`$ achieving

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left\{V_{1}^{\star,{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(m_{f})-V_{1}^{\widetilde{\mu},{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(m_{f})\leq\varepsilon\right\}\geq\frac{3}{4},`$ |  | (118) |

which, in view of
([117](#A5.E117 "In E.2.1 Step 1: Converting the goal to estimate (𝑓,ϕ) ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
indicates that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left\{\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}\right\}\geq\frac{3}{4}.`$ |  | (119) |

Consequently, taking
$`\widetilde{\phi}=\arg\min_{\phi\in\Phi}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}`$,
we construct the estimate of $`\phi`$ as
$`\widehat{\phi}=\widetilde{\phi}`$. If
$`\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}`$
for some $`\phi\in\Phi`$, then for any $`\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi`$ obeying
$`\phi^{\prime}\neq\phi`$, one has

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi^{\prime}}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\geq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}^{\star,f,\phi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi^{\prime}}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}-\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle>`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{H}{4}-\frac{H}{8}=\frac{H}{8},`$ |  | (120) |

where the first inequality holds due to the triangle inequality, and the
last inequality follows from the assumption
$`\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}`$
and the separation property of $`\phi\in\Phi`$; see
([84](#A5.E84 "In E.1.1 Constructing hard robust MDP instances ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")).
Similarly, we have $`\widehat{\phi}=\phi`$ if

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}<\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi^{\prime}}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1},\forall\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi,\phi^{\prime}\neq\phi,`$ |  | (121) |

which can be directly achieved when
$`\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}`$,
and further leads to

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left[\widehat{\phi}=\phi\right]\geq\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left\{\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}\right\}\geq\frac{3}{4}.`$ |  | (122) |

#### E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses

Next, we address the hypothesis testing problem over $`\phi\in\Phi`$ and
derive the information-theoretic lower bound for the probability of
error. Specifically, we define the minimax probability of error as:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
p_{\mathrm{e}}\coloneqq\inf_{(\widehat{f},\widehat{\phi})}\max_{(f,\phi)\in\mathcal{F}\times\Phi}\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\big(\widehat{\phi}\neq\phi\big),
``` |  |

where the infimum is taken over all possible tests $`\widehat{\phi}`$
constructed from the available batch dataset.

Given the dataset $`\mathcal{D}_{0}`$ with $`K`$ independent
trajectories, let $`\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\phi}`$ (and
$`\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\phi}_{h}(s,a)`$) represent the distribution
vector (and distribution) of each sample tuple
$`(s_{h},a_{h},s_{h}^{\prime})`$ at time step $`h`$ under the nominal
transition kernel $`P^{\star}`$ for
$`\mathcal{M}_{f}^{\phi,\mathsf{n}}`$. Then, employing Fano’s inequality
\[[36](#bib.bib2), Theorem 2.2\] and the additivity of KL divergence
\[[36](#bib.bib2), Page 85\], it follows that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{e}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq 1-K\frac{\mathop{\max}_{(\phi,\widetilde{\phi})\in\Phi,\phi\neq\widetilde{\phi}}\mathsf{KL}\big(\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\phi}\,|\,\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\widetilde{\phi}}\big)+\log 2}{\log|\Phi|}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\geq}1-\frac{8K}{H}\mathop{\max}_{(\phi,\widetilde{\phi})\in\Phi,\phi\neq\widetilde{\phi}}\mathsf{KL}\big(\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\phi}\,|\,\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\widetilde{\phi}}\big)-\frac{8\log 2}{H}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\geq}\frac{1}{2}-\frac{8K}{H}\max_{(\phi,\widetilde{\phi})\in\Phi,\phi\neq\widetilde{\phi}}\mathsf{KL}\big(\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\phi}\,|\,\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\widetilde{\phi}}\big),`$ |  | (123) |

where (i) holds due to $`|\Phi|\geq e^{H/8}`$ and (ii) follows from
$`H\geq 16\log 2`$.

Since the occupancy state distribution $`d^{\mathsf{n}}_{h}`$ is the
same for any MDP $`\mathcal{M}^{\phi}_{f},\,\forall\phi\in\Phi`$, we
apply the chain rule of KL divergence \[[12](#bib.bib33), Lemma 5.2.8\]
and the Markov property of the independent sample trajectories to
obtain:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}\big(\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\phi}\,|\,\varrho^{\mathsf{n},\widetilde{\phi}}\big)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{s\sim d_{h}^{\mathsf{n}}(s)}\left[\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\star,\phi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)\parallel P^{\star,\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)\big)\right]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{=}\frac{1}{2}\overline{\varrho}(m_{f})\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\left[\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\phi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\parallel P^{\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\big)\right],`$ |  | (124) |

where (i) follows from applying
([110](#A5.E110 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
and obtaining

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{s\sim d_{h}^{\mathsf{n}}(s)}\left[\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\star,\phi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)\parallel P^{\star,\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)\big)\right]`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s}d^{\mathsf{n}}_{h}(s)\left\{\sum_{a,s^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mu}^{\mathsf{n}}_{h}(a\,|\,s)P_{h}^{\phi_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)\log\frac{\widetilde{\mu}^{\mathsf{n}}_{h}(a\,|\,s)P_{h}^{\phi_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)}{\widetilde{\mu}^{\mathsf{n}}_{h}(a\,|\,s)P_{h}^{\widetilde{\phi}_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)}\right\}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\overline{\varrho}(m_{f})\sum_{a}\sum_{s^{\prime}}P_{h}^{\phi_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,m_{f},a)\log\frac{P_{h}^{\phi_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,m_{f},a)}{P_{h}^{\widetilde{\phi}_{h}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,m_{f},a)}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\overline{\varrho}(m_{f})\sum_{a}\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\phi_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\phi}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\big).`$ |  |

Consequently, combining
([123](#A5.E123 "In E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
and
([124](#A5.E124 "In E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
leads to

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{e}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{4K}{H}\max_{(\phi,\widetilde{\phi})\in\Phi,\phi\neq\widetilde{\phi}}\left[\overline{\varrho}(m_{f})\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{a}\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\phi_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\phi}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\big)\right].`$ |  | (125) |

We proceed to analyze
([125](#A5.E125 "In E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
by considering different cases of the uncertainty level
$`{\sigma^{+}}`$.

- •
  For $`0<{\sigma^{+}}\leq\frac{c_{2}}{2H}`$: If
  $`\phi_{h}=\widetilde{\phi}_{h}`$, it is obvious that

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\star,\phi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)\parallel P^{\star,\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)\big)=0.`$ |  | (126) |

  Consider the case of $`\phi_{h}\neq\widetilde{\phi}_{h}`$. Without
  loss of generality, we suppose $`\phi_{h}=0`$ and
  $`\widetilde{\phi}_{h}=1`$, which indicates

  |  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\star,\phi}_{h}(0\,|\,m_{f},0)\parallel P^{\star,\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(0\,|\,m_{f},0)\big)`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{(p-q)^{2}}{q(1-q)}\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{=}\frac{\Delta^{2}}{q(1-q)}`$ |  |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{=}\frac{(c_{5})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{H^{4}q(1-q)}\leq\frac{4(c_{5})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{c_{2}H^{3}},`$ |  | (127) |

  where the first inequality exists by applying
  Lemma [E.1](#A5.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma E.1. ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"), (i)
  follows from the definitions in
  ([104](#A5.E104 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")), (ii)
  holds due to the definition in
  ([115](#A5.E115 "In E.2.1 Step 1: Converting the goal to estimate (𝑓,ϕ) ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
  and the last inequality arises from $`q=p-\Delta\geq\frac{c_{2}}{2H}`$
  (see
  ([105](#A5.E105 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")))
  and $`1-q\geq 1-p\geq 1-\frac{c_{2}}{H}\geq\frac{1}{2}`$. Similarly,
  we establish the same bound for
  $`\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\star,\phi}_{h}(0\,|\,m_{f},1)\parallel P^{\star,\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(0\,|\,m_{f},1)\big)`$.
  Further incorporating
  ([127](#A5.Ex11 "In 1st item ‣ E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
  yields

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\star,\phi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\parallel P^{\star,\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\big)\leq\frac{16(c_{5})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{c_{2}H^{3}}.`$ |  | (128) |
- •
  For $`\frac{c_{2}}{2H}<{\sigma^{+}}\leq 1-c_{0}`$: Following the same
  pipeline, it then boils down to control the main term as below:

  |  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\star,\phi}_{h}(0\,|\,m_{f},0)\parallel P^{\star,\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(0\,|\,m_{f},0)\big)`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{(p-q)^{2}}{q(1-q)}\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{=}\frac{\Delta^{2}}{q(1-q)}`$ |  |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{=}\frac{(c_{5})^{2}{\sigma^{+}}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{H^{2}q(1-q)}\leq\frac{2(c_{5})^{2}{\sigma^{+}}\varepsilon^{2}}{c_{0}H^{2}},`$ |  | (129) |

  where (i) and (ii) follow from the definitions in
  ([104](#A5.E104 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
  and
  ([115](#A5.E115 "In E.2.1 Step 1: Converting the goal to estimate (𝑓,ϕ) ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")).
  Here, the last inequality arises from

  |  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle 1-q`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq 1-p=1-(1+\frac{c_{1}}{H}){\sigma^{+}}\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\geq}c_{0}-\frac{c_{1}}{H}\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\geq}\frac{c_{0}}{2}`$ |  |  |
  |  | $`\displaystyle p`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq q=p-\Delta\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\geq}{\sigma^{+}},`$ |  | (130) |

  where (ii) holds due to the definition of $`c_{1}=\frac{c_{0}}{2}`$,
  and (iii) follows from
  ([106](#A5.E106 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")).
  Consequently, we arrive at

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\star,\phi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,,s,a)\parallel P^{\star,\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,,s,a)\big)\leq\frac{8(c_{5})^{2}{\sigma^{+}}\varepsilon^{2}}{c_{0}H^{2}}.`$ |  | (131) |

Summing up
([128](#A5.E128 "In 1st item ‣ E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
and
([131](#A5.E131 "In 2nd item ‣ E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
we achieve for any $`(\phi,\widetilde{\phi})\in\Phi`$ with
$`\phi\neq\widetilde{\phi}`$ and any time step $`h\in[H]`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\mathsf{KL}\big(P^{\star,\phi}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\parallel P^{\star,\widetilde{\phi}}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\big)\leq\frac{16(c_{5})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{c_{0}c_{2}H^{2}}\max\{{\sigma^{+}},1/H\}.`$ |  | (132) |

Plugging
([132](#A5.E132 "In E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
back to
([125](#A5.E125 "In E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
under the definition in
([111](#A5.E111 "In E.1.4 Construction of the history/batch dataset ‣ E.1 Step 1: Constructing a family of hard Markov game instances ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
we obtain

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{e}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{4K}{H}\max_{(\phi,\widetilde{\phi})\in\Phi,\phi\neq\widetilde{\phi}}\left[\overline{\varrho}(m_{f})\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{a}\mathsf{KL}\big(P_{h}^{\phi_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\parallel P_{h}^{\widetilde{\phi}_{h}}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f},a)\big)\right]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{4K}{H}\overline{\varrho}(m_{f})\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{16(c_{5})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{c_{0}c_{2}H^{2}}\max\{{\sigma^{+}},1/H\}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{64K(c_{5})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{c_{0}c_{2}CSAH^{2}}\max\{{\sigma^{+}},1/H\}\geq\frac{1}{4},`$ |  | (133) |

as long as the sample size, $`T=KH`$, of the dataset is selected as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle T\leq\frac{c_{0}c_{2}CSAH^{3}\min\{1/{\sigma^{+}},H\}}{256(c_{5})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}\leq\frac{c_{0}c_{2}C_{\mathrm{r}}^{\star}SAH^{3}\min\{1/{\sigma^{+}},H\}}{256(c_{5})^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}.`$ |  | (134) |

#### E.2.3 Step 3: Putting all together

Next, we establish
([113](#A5.E113 "In E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
by contradiction. Suppose that there exists an estimator
$`\widetilde{\mu}`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\max_{(f,\phi\in\mathcal{F})\times\Phi}\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left[\left\{V_{1}^{\star,{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(\varrho)-V_{1}^{\widetilde{\mu},{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(\varrho)\right\}\geq\varepsilon\right]<\frac{1}{4}.`$ |  | (135) |

According to
([113](#A5.E113 "In E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
we would need

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall w\in\mathcal{F}:\quad\max_{\phi\in\Phi}\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left[\left\{V_{1}^{\star,{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(m_{f})-V_{1}^{\widetilde{\mu},{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(m_{f})\right\}\geq\varepsilon\right]<\frac{1}{4}.`$ |  | (136) |

To meet
([136](#A5.E136 "In E.2.3 Step 3: Putting all together ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
for any $`w\in\mathcal{F}`$, we would require

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall\phi\in\Phi:\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left\{V_{1}^{\star,{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(m_{f})-V_{1}^{\widetilde{\mu},{\sigma^{+}},f,\phi}(m_{f})<\varepsilon\right\}\geq\frac{3}{4},`$ |  | (137) |

which, in view of
([117](#A5.E117 "In E.2.1 Step 1: Converting the goal to estimate (𝑓,ϕ) ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
indicates that we would need

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall\phi\in\Phi:\quad\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left\{\sum_{h=1}^{H}\big\|\widetilde{\mu}_{h}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})-\widetilde{\mu}_{h}^{\star,f,\phi}(\cdot\,|\,m_{f})\big\|_{1}<\frac{H}{8}\right\}\geq\frac{3}{4}.`$ |  | (138) |

On the other hand,
([122](#A5.E122 "In E.2.1 Step 1: Converting the goal to estimate (𝑓,ϕ) ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
indicates

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall\phi\in\Phi:\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left[\widehat{\phi}=\phi\right]\geq\frac{3}{4}.`$ |  | (139) |

To achieve
([135](#A5.E135 "In E.2.3 Step 3: Putting all together ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
or, in other words,
([138](#A5.E138 "In E.2.3 Step 3: Putting all together ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
we apply
([139](#A5.E139 "In E.2.3 Step 3: Putting all together ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
to all $`w\in\mathcal{F}`$, which would require

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall(f,\phi)\in\mathcal{F}\times\Phi:\quad\mathbb{P}_{f,\phi}\left[(\widehat{f},\widehat{\phi})=(f,\phi)\right]\geq\frac{3}{4}.`$ |  | (140) |

This contract with
([133](#A5.Ex14 "In E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
as long as the sample size condition in
([134](#A5.E134 "In E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
is satisfied. Thus, if the sample size obeys
([134](#A5.E134 "In E.2.2 Step 2: Developing the probability of error in testing multiple hypotheses ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
we cannot achieve an estimate $`\widetilde{\mu}`$ that satisfies
([135](#A5.E135 "In E.2.3 Step 3: Putting all together ‣ E.2 Step 2: Establishing the minimax lower bound ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
which completes the proof.

## Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games

In this section, we extend RTZ-VI-LCB to the setting of multi-player
general-sum Markov games and present the corresponding theoretical
guarantees.

### F.1 Problem formulation

A robust general-sum Markov game is a tuple
$`\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m},H,\{\mathcal{U}_{\rho}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0})\}_{i=1}^{m},\{r_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m})`$
with $`m`$ players, where $`\mathcal{S}`$ denotes the state space and
$`H`$ is the horizon length. We have $`m`$ different action spaces,
where $`\mathcal{A}_{i}`$ is the action space for the $`i^{\text{th}}`$
player and $`|\mathcal{A}_{i}|=A_{i}`$. We let
$`\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{A}_{m}`$ denote
the joint action space, and let
$`\bm{a}:=(a_{1},\cdots,a_{m})\in\mathcal{A}`$ denote the joint actions
by all $`m`$ players. A notable deviation from standard MGs is that: for
$`1\leq i\leq m`$, instead of assuming a fixed transition kernel, each
$`i^{\text{th}}`$ player anticipates that the transition kernel is
allowed to be chosen arbitrarily from a prescribed uncertainty set
$`\mathcal{U}_{\rho}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0})`$. Here, the uncertainty set
$`\mathcal{U}_{\rho}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0})`$ is constructed centered on
$`P^{0}(\cdot|s,\bm{a})`$, with its size and shape defined by a certain
distance metric $`\rho`$ and a radius parameter $`\sigma_{i}>0`$.
$`r_{i}=\{r_{h,i}\}_{h\in[H]}`$ is a collection of reward functions for
the $`i^{\text{th}}`$ player, so that $`r_{h,i}(s,\bm{a})`$ gives the
reward received by the $`i^{\text{th}}`$ player if actions $`\bm{a}`$
are taken at state $`s`$ at step $`h`$.

The policy of the $`i^{\text{th}}`$ player is denoted as
$`\pi_{i}:=\big\{\pi_{h,i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\big\}_{h\in[H]}`$.
We denote the product policy of all players as
$`\pi:=\pi_{1}\times\cdots\times\pi_{M}`$, and denote the policy of all
players except the $`i^{\text{th}}`$ player as $`\pi_{-i}`$. We define
$`V^{\pi}_{h,i}(s)`$ as the expected cumulative reward that will be
received by the $`i^{\text{th}}`$ player if starting at state $`s`$ at
step $`h`$ and all players follow policy $`\pi`$. For any strategy
$`\pi_{-i}`$, there also exists a *robust best response* of the
$`i^{\text{th}}`$ player, which is a policy $`\mu^{\star}(\pi_{-i})`$
satisfying
$`V_{h,i}^{\mu^{\star}(\pi_{-i}),\pi_{-i},\sigma_{i}}(s)=\sup_{\pi_{i}}V_{h,i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i},\sigma_{i}}(s)`$
for any $`(s,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times[H]`$. For convenience, we denote
$`V_{h,i}^{\star,\pi_{-i},\sigma_{i}}:=V_{h,i}^{\mu^{\star}(\pi_{-i}),\pi_{-i},\sigma_{i}}`$.
The $`Q`$-functions of the robust best response can be defined
similarly.

Similar to the definition of behavior policy
$`(\mu^{\sf{n}},\nu^{\sf{n}})`$, we use the short-hand notation for the
occupancy distribution w.r.t. the behavior policy
$`\pi^{\sf{n}}=(\pi_{i}^{\sf{n}},\pi_{-i}^{\sf{n}})`$ as:
$`\forall(h,s,\bm{a})\in[H]\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  |  |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle d_{h}^{{\sf{n}},P^{0}}(s)`$ | $`\displaystyle=d_{h}^{\pi^{\sf{n}},P^{0}}(s)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}(s_{h}=s\,|\,s_{1}\sim\varrho^{\sf{n}},\pi^{\sf{n}},P^{0});`$ |  | (141a) |
|  | $`\displaystyle d_{h}^{{\sf{n}},P^{0}}(s,\bm{a})`$ | $`\displaystyle=d_{h}^{\pi^{\sf{n}},P^{0}}(s,\bm{a})\coloneqq\mathbb{P}(s_{h}=s\,|\,s_{1}\sim\varrho^{\sf{n}},\pi^{\sf{n}},P^{0})\,\pi^{\sf{n}}(\bm{a}\,|\,s).`$ |  | (141b) |

Similarly, for any product policy $`\pi=(\pi_{i},\pi_{-i})`$, there is,
$`\forall(h,s,\bm{a})\in[H]\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}`$

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  |  |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle d_{h}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i},P}(s)`$ | $`\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{P}(s_{h}=s\,|\,s_{1}\sim\varrho,\pi,P);`$ |  | (142a) |
|  | $`\displaystyle d_{h}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i},P}(s,\bm{a})`$ | $`\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{P}(s_{h}=s\,|\,s_{1}\sim\varrho,\pi,P)\,\pi_{i,h}(a_{i}\,|\,s)\,\pi_{-i,h}(\bm{a}_{-i}\,|\,s).`$ |  | (142b) |

Therefore, the robust variant of standard solution concepts—robust NE
for Robust multi-player general-sum MGs is introcuded as follows: A
product policy $`\pi`$ is considered a robust NE if

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall(s)\in\mathcal{S},\quad V_{1}^{\pi,\sigma_{i}}(s)=V_{h}^{\star,\pi_{-i},\sigma^{+}}(s).`$ |  | (143) |

A robust NE signifies that given the product policy $`(\pi)`$ of the
opponents, no player can enhance their outcome by deviating from their
current policy unilaterally when each player accounts for the worst-case
scenario within their uncertainty set
$`\mathcal{U}_{\rho}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0})`$ for all $`i=1,2,\cdots,m`$.

Since finding exact robust equilibria can be complex and may not always
be feasible, practitioners often seek approximate equilibria. In this
context, a product policy $`\pi\in\Delta(\mathcal{A})`$ can be termed an
$`\varepsilon`$-robust NE if

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
{\mathrm{Gap}({\pi})\coloneqq\max\left\{\left\{V_{i,1}^{\star,\pi_{-i},\sigma_{i}}(\varrho)-V_{i,1}^{\pi,\sigma_{i}}(\varrho)\right\}_{i=1}^{m}\right\}\leq\varepsilon,}
``` |  | (144) |

where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{1}^{\star,\pi_{-i},\sigma_{i}}(\varrho)=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\varrho}V_{1}^{\star,\pi_{-i},\sigma_{i}}(s),\qquad\text{and}\qquad V_{1}^{\star,\sigma_{i}}(\varrho)=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\varrho}V_{1}^{\star,\sigma_{i}}(s).`$ |  |

The existence of robust NE has been established for general divergence
functions used in the uncertainty set in \[[5](#bib.bib29)\].

##### Goal

With a dataset collected from the nominal environment, our objective is
to find a solution among the $`\varepsilon`$-robust NEs for the robust
multi-player general-sum MG $`\mathcal{MG}_{\mathsf{r}}`$ w.r.t. a
specified uncertainty set $`\mathcal{U}_{\rho}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0})`$
around the nominal kernel, while minimizing the number of samples
required under partial coverage of the state-action space.

### F.2 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB

Here we present the Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB algorithm in
Algorithm [4](#alg4 "Algorithm 4 ‣ F.2 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
which is an extension of
Algorithm [2](#alg2 "Algorithm 2 ‣ Policy estimation. ‣ 3.2 Optimistic Variant of Robust Value Iteration with Lower Confidence Bounds ‣ 3 Algorithm Design ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")
for multi-player general-sum Markov games.

According to the empirical frequencies of state transitions, we can
naturally construct an empirical estimate
$`\widehat{P}^{0}=\{\widehat{P}^{0}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}`$ of $`P^{0}`$,
where

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{P}_{h}^{0}\left(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,\bm{a}\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\ind\left\{\left(s_{j},\bm{a}_{j},s_{j}^{\prime}\right)=\left(s,\bm{a},s^{\prime}\right)\right\},&\text{if }N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)>0;\\
\frac{1}{S},&\text{if }N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)=0,\end{cases}
``` |  | (145) |

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{r}_{i,h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)=\begin{cases}r_{i,h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right),&\text{if }N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)>0;\\
0,&\text{if }N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)=0,\end{cases}
``` |  | (146) |

for any
$`(i,h,s,\bm{a},s^{\prime})\in[m]\times[H]\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{S}`$.
Besides, $`N_{h}(s,\bm{a})`$ represents the total number of sample
transitions from $`(s,\bm{a})`$ at step $`h`$, and

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle N_{h}(s,\bm{a})`$ | $`\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathds{1}\big\{(s_{j},\bm{a}_{j})=(s,\bm{a})\big\}.`$ |  | (147) |

Before the details of Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB, we extend
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 Building an Empirical Nominal MDP ‣ 3 Algorithm Design ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")
as
Algorithm [3](#alg3 "Algorithm 3 ‣ F.2 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
which reduces statistical dependencies and produces a distributionally
equivalent dataset $`\mathcal{D}_{0}`$ with independent samples. Similar
to
Lemma [3.1](#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3.1 Building an Empirical Nominal MDP ‣ 3 Algorithm Design ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
we present the following lemma concerning the dataset
$`\mathcal{D}_{0}`$, whose proof is similar to the context in
Appendix [C](#A3 "Appendix C Proof of Lemma ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning").

0:  Dataset $`\mathcal{D}`$, probability $`\delta`$.

1:  Step 1: Data Partitioning. Split $`\mathcal{D}`$ into two
equal-sized subsets, $`\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}}`$ and
$`\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{a}}`$, each containing $`K/2`$ trajectories.

2:  Step 2: Defining Transition Bounds. For step $`h`$ and state $`s`$,
denote the number of transitions from $`\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}}`$
(resp. $`\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{a}}`$) as $`N^{\mathsf{m}}_{h}(s)`$ (resp.
$`N^{\mathsf{a}}_{h}(s)`$). Construct the trimmed count as:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
N^{\mathsf{t}}_{h}(s)\coloneqq\max\left\{N^{\mathsf{a}}_{h}(s)-10\sqrt{N^{\mathsf{a}}_{h}(s)\log\frac{HS}{\delta}},\,0\right\}.
``` |  |

3:  Step 3: Generating Subsampled Dataset. Randomly sample transitions
(quadruples of the form $`(s,\bm{a},h,s^{\prime})`$) from
$`\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}}`$ uniformly. For each
$`(s,h)\in\mathcal{S}\times[H]`$, include
$`\min\{N^{\mathsf{t}}_{h}(s),N^{\mathsf{m}}_{h}(s)\}`$ transitions in
the new dataset $`\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{t}}`$.

3:  Set $`\mathcal{D}_{0}=\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{t}}`$.

Algorithm 3 Two-stage subsampling for Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB.

###### Lemma F.1.

The dataset produced by the two-stage subsampling method is
distributionally identical to $`\mathcal{D}_{0}`$ with probability at
least $`1-8\delta`$, where $`\{N_{h}(s,\bm{a})\}`$ are independent of
the sample transitions in $`\mathcal{D}^{0}`$ and obey:
$`\forall(h,s,\bm{a})\in[H]\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
N_{h}(s,\bm{a})\geq\frac{Kd^{\mathsf{n}}_{h}(s,\bm{a})}{8}-5\sqrt{Kd^{\mathsf{n}}_{h}(s,\bm{a})\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}.
``` |  | (148) |

1:  Initialization: Set uncertainty levels $`\sigma_{i}`$ for
$`i=1,2,\cdots,m`$; set $`\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s)=H`$ and
$`\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s,\bm{a})=H`$ for all
$`(i,s,\bm{a},h)\in[m]\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\left[H+1\right]`$.

2:  Compute the empirical reward function $`\widehat{r}`$ using
([146](#A6.E146 "In F.2 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
and the empirical transition kernel $`\widehat{P}_{0}`$ using
([145](#A6.E145 "In F.2 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")).

3:  for $`h=H,H-1,\ldots,1`$ do

4:   Update the robust Q-value estimate as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\min\left\{\widehat{r}_{i,h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)+\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}\left(\widehat{P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}\right)}P\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}+\beta_{i,h}\left(s,\bm{a},\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\right),\,H\right\},`$ |  |

with
$`\beta_{i,h}\left(s,\bm{a},V\right)=\min\left\{\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{C_{\mathsf{n}}\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}}(V)},\frac{2C_{\mathsf{n}}H\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)}\right\},H\right\}.`$

5:   Compute Nash policy for each $`s\in\mathcal{S}`$ as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\pi_{h}\left(s\right)=\left(\pi_{i,h}\left(s\right),\pi_{-i,h}\left(s\right)\right)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathsf{ComputNash}\left(\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}\left(s,\cdot\right)\right),`$ |  |

6:   Update the robust value estimate for each $`s\in\mathcal{S}`$ as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}\left(s\right)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim\pi_{h}\left(s\right)}\left[\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)\right].`$ |  |

7:  end for

7:  The product policy
$`\hat{\pi}\left(s\right)=\{\pi_{h}\left(s\right)\}_{h=1}^{H}`$ with
$`{\pi}_{h}\left(s\right)=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\pi_{i,h}\left(s\right)`$.

Algorithm 4 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB.

Based on
Algorithm [4](#alg4 "Algorithm 4 ‣ F.2 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
we propose a model-based approach for solving robust multi-player
general-sum MGs using an approximate $`\widehat{P}^{0}`$ for $`P^{0}`$,
as summarized in
Algorithm [4](#alg4 "Algorithm 4 ‣ F.2 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning").

Similar to
([16](#S3.E16 "In Dual problem. ‣ 3.2 Optimistic Variant of Robust Value Iteration with Lower Confidence Bounds ‣ 3 Algorithm Design ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
we can tackle the multi-player general-sum MGs problem as:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}\left(\widehat{P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}\right)}\!\!P\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\!=\!\max_{\alpha\in[\min_{s}\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}},\max_{s}\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}]}\!\Big\{\widehat{P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}\!\left[\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\right]_{\alpha}\!\!-\!\sigma_{i}\left(\alpha\!-\!\min_{s^{\prime}}\left[\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\right]_{\alpha}\!(s^{\prime})\right)\!\Big\}.`$ |  | (149) |

where $`\left[\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\right]_{\alpha}`$
respectively denote the clipped versions of
$`\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{S}`$ based on some
level $`\alpha\geq 0`$, as follows.

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left[\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\right]_{\alpha}(s)`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}(s),&\text{if }\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}(s)>\alpha;\\
\alpha.&\text{otherwise;}\end{cases}`$ |  | (150) |

### F.3 Analysis of Multi-ME-Nash-QL

In this subsection, we prove
Theorem [4.5](#S4.Thmtheorem5 "Theorem 4.5 (Upper bound for Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB). ‣ Robust unilateral clipped concentrability. ‣ 4 Performance Guarantees ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
which can separated into three steps as the proof of
Theorem [4.2](#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Upper bound for RTZ-VI-LCB). ‣ Robust unilateral clipped concentrability. ‣ 4 Performance Guarantees ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning").

First of all, similar to
Assumption [4.1](#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Definition 4.1 (Robust unilateral clipped concentrability). ‣ Robust unilateral clipped concentrability. ‣ 4 Performance Guarantees ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
we measure the distributional discrepancy between the historical data
and the target data to assess the effectiveness of the historical
dataset for achieving the desired goal. We propose a novel assumption
for robust multi-agent general-sum MGs as:

###### Assumption F.2 (Robust multiple clipped concentrability).

The behavior policies of the historical dataset $`\mathcal{D}`$
satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\{\sup_{(\pi_{-i},s,\bm{a},h,P)\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{-i})\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times[H]\times\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0})}\frac{\min\big\{d_{h}^{\pi_{i}^{\star},\pi_{-i},P}(s,\bm{a}),\frac{1}{S\sum_{i=1}^{m}A_{i}}\big\}}{d_{h}^{\mathsf{n},P^{0}}(s,\bm{a})}\right\}_{i=1}^{m}\right\}\leq C^{\star}_{\mathrm{mr}}`$ |  | (151) |

#### F.3.1 Step 1: decoupling statistical dependency

Before bounding $`\mathrm{Gap}(\widehat{\pi})`$, we introduce an
important lemma whose proof is similar to
Lemma [D.1](#A4.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma D.1. ‣ D.1 Step 1: Decoupling statistical dependency ‣ Appendix D Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")
in
Appendix [G.1](#A7.SS1 "G.1 Proof of Lemma ‣ Appendix G Proofs of lemmas for Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
quantifying the difference between $`\widehat{P}`$ and $`P`$ when
projected in the direction of the value function.

###### Lemma F.3.

Instate the assumptions in
Theorem [4.5](#S4.Thmtheorem5 "Theorem 4.5 (Upper bound for Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB). ‣ Robust unilateral clipped concentrability. ‣ 4 Performance Guarantees ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning").
Consider any vector $`V\in\mathbb{R}^{S}`$ with $`\|V\|_{\infty}\leq H`$
for all
$`(i,h,s,\bm{a})\in[m]\times[H]\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}`$
satisfying $`N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)>0`$. With probability at least
$`1-\delta`$, one has

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|\inf_{{P}\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}(\widehat{P}_{h,s,\bm{a}}^{0})}PV-\inf_{{P}\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}})}PV\right|\leq C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)}\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}}\big({V}\big)\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}+C_{4}\frac{H\log\frac{KH}{\delta}}{N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)}`$ |  | (152) |

for some sufficiently large constant $`C_{4}>0`$, and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathsf{Var}_{\widehat{P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}}\big({V}\big)\leq 2\mathsf{Var}_{P^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}}\big({V}\big)+O\left(\frac{H^{2}}{N_{h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right)}\log\frac{KH}{\delta}\right).
``` |  | (153) |

With
Lemma [F.3](#A6.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma F.3. ‣ F.3.1 Step 1: decoupling statistical dependency ‣ F.3 Analysis of Multi-ME-Nash-QL ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
we can now have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left|\inf_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}(\widehat{P}_{h,s,\bm{a}}^{0})}PV-\inf_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}})}PV\right|\leq\beta_{h}\left(s,\bm{a},{V}\right)
``` |  | (154) |

for any
$`(i,h,s,\bm{a})\in[m]\times[H]\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}`$
satisfying $`N_{h}(s,\bm{a})\geq 1`$.

Therefore, we conclude that $`\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s,\bm{a})`$
is an optimistic estimation of
$`\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi,\sigma_{i}}(s,\bm{a})`$ for any
$`i=1,2,\cdots,m`$, which is summarized below, whose proof is similar to
Lemma [D.2](#A4.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma D.2. ‣ D.1 Step 1: Decoupling statistical dependency ‣ Appendix D Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")
in
Appendix [G.2](#A7.SS2 "G.2 Proof of Lemma ‣ Appendix G Proofs of lemmas for Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning").

###### Lemma F.4.

With probability exceeding $`1-\delta`$, it holds that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s,\bm{a})\geq{Q}_{i,h}^{\star,\widehat{\pi}_{-i},\sigma_{i}}(s,\bm{a})\qquad`$ | $`\displaystyle\text{and}\qquad\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s)\geq{V}_{i,h}^{\star,\widehat{\pi}_{-i},\sigma_{i}}(s).`$ |  | (155) |

Besides, we introduce another key lemma highlighting the difference
between robust multi-player general-sum MGs and standard multi-player
general-sum MGs from the same idea of
Lemma [D.3](#A4.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma D.3. ‣ D.1 Step 1: Decoupling statistical dependency ‣ Appendix D Proof of Theorem ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
as shown below.

###### Lemma F.5.

Consider any multi-player general-sum MGs
$`\mathcal{MG}_{\mathsf{r}}=\big\{\mathcal{S},\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m},H,\{\mathcal{U}_{\rho}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0})\}_{i=1}^{m},\{r_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}\big\}`$
and the uncertainty set
$`\{\mathcal{U}_{\rho}^{\sigma_{i}}(P^{0})\}_{i=1}^{m}(\cdot)`$ with TV
distance. The optimistic robust value function estimate
$`\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\forall(i,h)\in[m]\times[H]:\quad\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}-\min_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{\left(H+1\right)\left(1-(1-\sigma_{i})^{H-h}\right)}{\sigma_{i}},H\right\}.`$ |  |

#### F.3.2 Step 2: decomposing the error $`\mathrm{Gap}(\widehat{\pi})`$

The goal of our algorithm is to output an $`\varepsilon`$-robust NE
policy $`(\widehat{\pi})`$ satisfying $`\mathrm{Gap}(\widehat{\pi})`$ in
([144](#A6.E144 "In F.1 Problem formulation ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
i.e.,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{Gap}(\widehat{\pi})\coloneqq\max\left\{\left\{V_{i,1}^{\star,\widehat{\pi}_{-i},\sigma_{i}}(\varrho)-V_{i,1}^{\widehat{\pi},\sigma_{i}}(\varrho)\right\}_{i=1}^{m}\right\}\leq\varepsilon.`$ |  |

According to the relationship in
Lemma [F.4](#A6.Thmtheorem4 "Lemma F.4. ‣ F.3.1 Step 1: decoupling statistical dependency ‣ F.3 Analysis of Multi-ME-Nash-QL ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
under the definition of
$`\mathcal{A}_{-i}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{A}_{i-1}\times\mathcal{A}_{i+1}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{A}_{m}`$,
we obtain

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle{V}_{h}^{\star,\widehat{\pi}_{-i,h},\sigma^{+}}(s)\leq\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s)=`$ | $`\displaystyle\min_{\max_{{\pi_{-i}}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{-i})}}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim({\pi_{i}}(s),{\pi}_{-i}(s))}\left[{Q}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s,\bm{a})\right],`$ |  | (156) |

where the first equality comes from line 8 in
Algorithm [4](#alg4 "Algorithm 4 ‣ F.2 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning").
Therefore, there exists a deterministic policy
$`\pi_{-i}^{\sf{d}}:\mathcal{S}\leftarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{-i})`$
satisfying that for any $`s\in\mathcal{S}`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\pi_{-i}^{\sf{d}}(s)\geq\arg\min_{{{\pi_{-i}}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim({\pi_{i}}(s),{\pi}_{-i}(s))}\left[{Q}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s,\bm{a})\right].`$ |  | (157) |

Before starting, we introduce several useful notations:

- •
  The state-action space covered by the behavior policy
  $`\pi^{\mathsf{n}}`$ in the nominal transition kernel $`P^{0}`$ is
  denoted as

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\mathsf{n}}=\left\{(h,s,\bm{a}):d_{h}^{\mathsf{n}}(s,\bm{a})>0\right\}.`$ |  | (158) |
- •
  For any $`(i,h)\in[m]\times[H]`$, the set of potential state occupancy
  distributions w.r.t. the policy
  $`(\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s))`$ and the uncertainty set
  $`P\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}\left(P^{0}\right)`$ f is denoted as

  |  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{pi}}_{i,h}`$ | $`\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\left[d_{h}^{\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s),P}(s)\right]_{s\in\mathcal{S}}:P\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}\left(P^{0}\right)\right\};`$ |  | (159) |
  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{pai}}_{i,h}`$ | $`\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\left[d_{h}^{\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s),P}(s,\bm{a})\right]_{(s,\bm{a})\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}:P\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}\left(P^{0}\right)\right\}.`$ |  | (160) |
- •
  For convenience and without ambiguity, we introduce an additional
  notation for $`(i,h)\in[m]\times[H]`$ as

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \beta_{i,h}^{\pi_{i},{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim(\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s))}\beta_{i,h}\left(s,\bm{a},\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\right).
  ``` |  |

  In particular, the vector
  $`\beta_{i,h}^{\pi_{i},{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{S}`$ is
  defined with its $`s`$-th item given by
  $`\beta_{i,h}^{\pi_{i},{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}}(s)`$.
- •
  Similarly, we can define the notation related to rewards for
  $`(i,h)\in[m]\times[H]`$ as

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \widehat{r}_{i,h}^{\pi_{i},{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim(\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s))}\widehat{r}_{i,h}\left(s,\bm{a}\right).
  ``` |  |

To proceed with the analysis, we need to introduce a pessimistic
V-estimation $`\underline{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s)`$ that are defined
similarly as $`\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s)`$. Similar to
Lemma [F.4](#A6.Thmtheorem4 "Lemma F.4. ‣ F.3.1 Step 1: decoupling statistical dependency ‣ F.3 Analysis of Multi-ME-Nash-QL ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"),
we have
$`\underline{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s)\leq{V}_{i,h}^{\widehat{\pi},\sigma_{i}}(s)`$.

Therefore, according to the update rule in line 4 in
Algorithm [4](#alg4 "Algorithm 4 ‣ F.2 Multi-RTZ-VI-LCB ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")
and robust Bellman equality similar to
([28](#A2.E28 "In B.2 Facts of RTZMGs and empirical RTZMGs ‣ Appendix B Preliminaries ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning")),
we derive

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle V_{i,h}^{\star,\widehat{\pi}_{-i},\sigma^{+}}(s)-V_{i,h}^{\widehat{\pi},\sigma^{+}}(s)`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s)-\underline{V}_{i,h}^{\sigma_{i}}(s)(s)`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim(\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s))}\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}\big(\widehat{P}_{h,s,\bm{a}}^{0}\big)}P\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}+2\beta_{i,h}^{\pi_{i},{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}}(s)-\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim(\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s))}\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{{\sigma_{i}}}\left({P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}\right)}P\underline{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim(\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s))}\Bigg[\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}\big({P}_{h,s,\bm{a}}^{0}\big)}P\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}-\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{{\sigma_{i}}}\left({P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}\right)}P\underline{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\left|\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{{\sigma_{i}}}\left({P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}\right)}\!P\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}-\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}\big(\widehat{P}_{h,s,\bm{a}}^{0}\big)}P\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\right|\Bigg]+2\beta_{i,h}^{\pi_{i},{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}}(s)`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim(\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s))}\left[\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{\sigma_{i}}\big({P}_{h,s,\bm{a}}^{0}\big)}P\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}-\inf_{P\in\mathcal{U}^{{\sigma_{i}}}\left({P}^{0}_{h,s,\bm{a}}\right)}P\underline{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\right]+3\beta_{i,h}^{\pi_{i},{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}}(s)`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}\sim(\pi_{i}(s),{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}(s))}\left[{P}^{\inf,V}_{i,h,s,\bm{a}}\left(\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}-\underline{V}_{i,h+1}^{\sigma_{i}}\right)\right]+3\beta_{i,h}^{\pi_{i},{\pi}_{-i}^{\sf{b}}}(s).`$ |  | (161) |

Here, (i) in
([161](#A6.E161 "In F.3.2 Step 2: decomposing the error Gap(𝜋̂) ‣ F.3 Analysis of Multi-ME-Nash-QL ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
exists due to
([154](#A6.E154 "In F.3.1 Step 1: decoupling statistical dependency ‣ F.3 Analysis of Multi-ME-Nash-QL ‣ Appendix F Multiplayer General-sum Markov Games ‣ Sample-Efficient Tabular Self-Play forOffline Robust Reinforcement Learning"))
in
````
